**Arm gemini-3.1-flash-lite_captured-low-r3** — TTFT 1.4 s, 98 words → **acceptable** (correctness 2 · on_topic 2 · delivery 1)

> I would use a combination of a hash map and a doubly linked list. The hash map stores the key mapped to the node in the linked list, which gives us O(1) lookups. > I choose read replicas when the bottleneck is read-heavy and query traffic is scaling, since they let you offload reads from the primary node. ### R17 · reasoning · 9 words · clip 3.9 s · first detection +2.4 s from the end of the clip · coverage 100 %

> How does target leakage sneak into a feature pipeline?

**In-app answer 1** — live, +2.6 s → **acceptable** (correctness 2 · on_topic 2 · delivery 1)
_grader: Accurate leakage paths and prevention, but runs well over 85 words._

heard: "How does target leakage sneak into a feature pipeline?"

> Target leakage usually sneaks in through global aggregations calculated across the entire dataset before splitting, or by accidentally including post-event indicators that wouldn't exist at inference time. For instance, when I built churn and risk models, if you compute rolling metrics using data from the whole timeline before your train-test split, the target variable leaks straight into the features. I prevent this by enforcing strict point-in-time correctness, encapsulating feature engineering inside isolated transformation steps, and validating that all feature timestamps strictly precede the label.
> 
> 

**Arm gemini-3.1-flash-lite_captured-low-r2** — TTFT 3.8 s, 79 words → **acceptable** (correctness 2 · on_topic 2 · delivery 2)
_grader: Post-event features and pre-split global statistics are the right leakage examples._

> Target leakage usually sneaks in when you include information that wouldn't be available at the time of prediction. The most common culprit is using post-event data, like including a 'churn_date' feature in a churn model. It also happens during preprocessing, like calculating global statistics on the entire dataset before splitting, which leaks test set information into your training features. I prevent this by enforcing strict time-based splits and ensuring all feature engineering pipelines are fitted exclusively on training data.
